L595: ## Rationale for the Applicant-Proposed Breakpoints by Pathogen
L596: The Applicant-proposed breakpoints for delafloxacin are summarized below by each target ABSSSI pathogens. According to the Applicant, the proposed breakpoints are based on MIC distribution, available PD modeling data including probability of target attainment analysis, and resulting clinical study outcomes as they correlate to delafloxacin MIC. The disk diffusion breakpoints were based on minimizing categorical error rates in accordance to CLSI M23 guidelines19.
L597: ## *Staphylococcus aureus*
L598: The Applicant proposed the following breakpoints for *S. aureus*, including MRSA and MSSA isolates:
L599: Organism	MIC (\mu\text{g/mL})	Zone Diametera (mm)
S. aureus
(methicillin-resistant and
methicillin-susceptible isolates)	(b) (4) Susceptible	(b) (4) Susceptible
(b) (4) Intermediate	Intermediate
(b) (4) Resistant	Resistant
L600: MIC=minimum inhibitory concentration
L601: a Delafloxacin 5-\mu\text{g} disk
L602: The following rationale was provided by the Applicant:
L603: - A susceptible breakpoint of \le (b) (4) \mu\text{g/mL} is predicted to cover 94.9% of contemporary *S. aureus* isolates and 96.4% of contemporary *S. aureus* isolates from skin and soft-tissue infections.
L604: Name of the Product: Baxdela
L605: (Delafoxacin 450 mg Tablets and IV 300 mg/vial)
L606: Date Review Completed: 06/15/2017
L607: - A susceptible breakpoint of \le 0.25 mcg/mL is predicted to cover 88.7% of contemporary MRSA isolates and 92.7% of contemporary MRSA isolates from skin and soft-tissue infections.
- Delafoxacin MICs are bimodal for *S. aureus* isolates and MRSA isolates presumably due to the existing resistance to levofloxacin. However, delafoxacin maintains activity against fluoroquinolone-resistant *S. aureus* and exhibited high rates of clinical success and microbiological eradication for fluoroquinolone-resistant isolates during Phase 3 clinical studies. The first mode (MIC values of 0.002-0.03 mcg/mL) had 98.2% (166/169) microbiological response rates in the MEFUI population. The second mode (MIC range of 0.06 to 4 mcg/mL) had similar efficacy rates of 98.7% (78/79).
- For subjects with *S. aureus* clinical isolates that were non-susceptible to levofloxacin, 98.8% (80/81) efficacy was observed. For subjects with MRSA isolates, similar efficacy (98.6% [70/71]) was observed in the MEFUI population.
- For *S. aureus* clinical isolates, the predominant mutation observed was Ser84-Leu in *gyrA*/Ser80-Phe in *parC*. The delafoxacin MIC90 for isolates with this mutation was 0.25 mcg/mL whereas the MIC90 for levofloxacin and ciprofloxacin was 8 and &gt; 8 mcg/mL. The microbiological response rate for isolates with this mutation was 98.8% (83/84).
- Based on achieving the PK/PD target, delafoxacin MIC value of 0.25 mcg/mL was predicted to be associated with 95% and 100% target attainment for IV 300 mg Q12h and IV 450 mg Q12h, respectively.
- The percent probability of target attainment associated with net bacterial stasis was 96.5% for all *S. aureus* isolates including 92.8% and 99.2% for MRSA and MSSA isolates, respectively. The percent probabilities of PK/PD target attainment for *S. aureus* at an MIC value of 0.25 mcg/mL were 90.8% and 86.7% on treatment Days 1 and 4, respectively.
- The Applicant has proposed an intermediate MIC BP of 0.5 mcg/mL for *S. aureus* and related species. However, no isolate was recovered with this MIC-level in clinical studies; therefore efficacy information is not available at this time.
L608: *Reviewer's comment: Against all S. aureus isolates (including both MRSA and MSSA) from clinical and surveillance studies, the MIC90 of delafoxacin was \le 0.25 mcg/mL. There were only 4 S. aureus (3 MRSA and 1 MSSA) isolates with an MIC 0.5 mcg/mL in the delafoxacin arm (319 subjects with baseline S. aureus isolates) in clinical studies. Among them, 3(75%) isolates were presumed eradicated and 1(25%) isolate persisted at the end of treatment.*
L609: *The Applicant-proposed susceptible breakpoint of 0.25 mcg/mL would be inappropriate as there is limited clinical data at this MIC. A susceptible breakpoint of 0.25 mcg/mL would be more appropriate based on in vitro MIC data, clinical experience and microbiological eradication at the end of treatment. The PK/PD target attainment data were also supportive in determining the S. aureus breakpoints. Monte Carlo simulation analysis predicted that almost 100% of simulated patients will achieve target attainment at an MIC value of 0.25 mcg/mL. This will allow setting the intermediate BP at 0.5 mcg/mL which will provide target attainment of &gt; 90\% and will accommodate inter laboratory testing variability related with AST.*
L610: Name of the Product: Baxdela
L611: (Delafoxacin 450 mg Tablets and IV 300 mg/vial)
L612: Date Review Completed: 06/15/2017
L613: *Based on in vitro MIC data, clinical experience and presumed microbiological eradication at the end of treatment, and PTA, this Reviewer recommends MIC BPs of S/I/R as \le 0.25/0.5/\ge 1 mcg/mL and disk diffusion BPs as \ge 23/20-22/\le 19 mm (Figure G13 in Appendix G).*

L634: Name of the Product: Baxdela

L636: Date Review Completed: 06/15/2017
L637: - A susceptible breakpoint of \le (b) (4) mcg/mL is predicted to cover 100% of contemporary *S. pyogenes* isolates and 100% of contemporary *S. pyogenes* isolates from skin and soft-tissue infections.
- The highest delafloxacin MIC value among surveillance isolates of *S. pyogenes* was 0.06 mcg/mL.

L638: Reviewer's comment: The MIC_{90} for delafloxacin against *S. pyogenes* isolates from clinical (n=46) and surveillance studies (n=902) were 0.06 and 0.015 mcg/mL respectively. The MIC ranged from 0.008-0.06 mcg/mL in the clinical studies and \le 0.004-0.06 mcg/mL in the surveillance studies. The pre-clinical PK-PD data were not available for *S. pyogenes*, however, the Applicant presumed that it would be similar to *S. aureus*.
L639: In absence of pre-clinical PK-PD data, this reviewer relied on *in vitro* MIC data, clinical experience and presumed microbiological eradication at the end of treatment, to determine the breakpoints for *S. pyogenes*. The Agency recommends a susceptible only breakpoint at \le 0.06 mcg/mL for *S. pyogenes*. No resistant breakpoints are proposed since clinical and surveillance data for these isolates indicate the absence or rare occurrence of resistant isolates. Therefore, isolates above the susceptible breakpoint should be reported as non-susceptible. Thus, only a susceptible breakpoint has been recommended. This Reviewer recommends a susceptible only disk diffusion zone diameter of \ge 20 mm (Figure G17 in Appendix G).

L665: Based on in vitro MIC data, clinical experience and presumed microbiological eradication at the end of treatment, a susceptible only BP of \le 0.03 mcg/mL would be more appropriate. In a follow up communication with the Applicant dated 6/7/2017, the Applicant requested to consider a susceptible BP of \le 0.06 mcg/mL as using an MIC BP of 0.06 mcg/mL, the potential for a false susceptible disk result would decrease. Using the MIC BP of 0.03 mcg/mL, 2.2% of the evaluated isolates would have been interpreted as susceptible by disk and non-susceptible by broth microdilution. No such discordance would be observed using a broth microdilution breakpoint of 0.06 mcg/mL. The Agency accepted this rationale.
L666: No resistant breakpoints are proposed since clinical and surveillance data for these isolates indicate the absence or rare occurrence of resistant isolates. Therefore, this reviewer recommends that isolates above the susceptible breakpoint should sent to the reference laboratory for further testing.
L667: This Reviewer recommends the susceptible only MIC BP of 0.06 mcg/mL and the disk diffusion susceptible BP of \ge 25 mm for *S. anginosus* Group (Figure G18 in Appendix G).

L687: The following rationale was provided by the Applicant:
L688: - A susceptible breakpoint of \le 0.25 mcg/mL is predicted to cover 66.6% of contemporary *E. coli* isolates and 68.2% of contemporary *E. coli* isolates from skin and soft-tissue infections.
- A susceptible breakpoint of \le 0.25 mcg/mL would be predicted to cover 20.2% of contemporary ESBL *E. coli* isolates and 15.6 % of contemporary ESBL *E. coli* isolates from skin and soft-tissue infections.
- Delafloxacin MIC distributions are bimodal for *E. coli* presumably due to existing resistance to levofloxacin via QRDR mutations and expression of efflux pumps along with frequently concomitant extended-spectrum beta-lactamases.
- However, clinical study data demonstrated that delafloxacin had 100% efficacy against ESBL-positive *E. coli* isolates with delafloxacin MIC values as high as 2 mcg/mL and 1 fluoroquinolone non-susceptible isolate. The proposed delafloxacin breakpoint of 0.25
L689: Name of the Product: Baxdela
L690: (Delafoxacin 450 mg Tablets and IV 300 mg/vial)
L691: Date Review Completed: 06/15/2017
L692: mcg/mL is predicted to cover *E. coli* isolates in the first mode but would declare isolates in the second mode as delafoxacin-resistant, where isolates already resistant to fluoroquinolones or possible fluoroquinolone resistance concomitant with pre-existing ESBLs would be more likely to be encountered.
L693: - When microbiological outcomes were tabulated by MIC for clinical study isolates (MEFUI population, Pool 1), 100% efficacy (11/11) of delafoxacin was observed for isolates up to a delafoxacin MIC of 2 mcg/mL.
- For *E. coli* clinical isolates, the distribution of isolates was bimodal. The first mode (MIC values of 0.008 to 0.06 mcg/mL) had 100% (9/9) microbiological response rates. The second mode (MIC range of 1 to 2 mcg/mL) had similar efficacy rates of 100% (2/2) (MEFUI population, Pool 1). Two of the isolates in the second mode were shown to be ESBL-positive and 1 isolate was levofloxacin non-susceptible.
- Efficacy of delafoxacin was demonstrated in a neutropenic mouse thigh model of infection with *E. coli* isolates with delafoxacin MIC values of 0.016 and 0.25 mcg/mL.
- The results suggested that bacterial stasis would be achieved at a fAUC24/MIC ratio of 14.5, and a 1-log10 bacterial reduction would be achieved at a fAUC24/MIC ratio of 26.2.
- The human PK of delafoxacin as determined in a Phase 3 study in patients with ABSSSI (RX-3341-302), was used to estimate the percent probability of fAUC24/MIC target attainment with delafoxacin 300 mg IV, BID, over a wide range of MIC values. The stasis target has been previously shown to be predictive of clinical efficacy in patients with ABSSSI. For bacterial stasis, the probability of mean target attainment was \ge 97.8\% for MIC values up to and including 0.25 mcg/mL.
- When microbiological outcomes were tabulated by MIC for clinical study isolates, 100% eradication (11/11) of delafoxacin was observed for *E. coli* isolates up to a delafoxacin MIC of 2 mcg/mL.
- The Applicant proposed an intermediate BP of 0.5 mcg/mL for *E. coli* and other Enterobacteriaceae. However, no *E. coli*, or *K. pneumoniae* isolate was recovered at this MIC-level in clinical studies.
L694: *Reviewer's comment: The clinical experience with E. coli and other indicated gram-negative pathogens was very limited in clinical studies. While E. coli was recovered at baseline from 14 subjects in delafoxacin arm in clinical studies, they were mostly associated with polymicrobial infections. The MIC90 of delafoxacin against E. coli isolates from both clinical and surveillance studies were 4 mcg/mL. The MIC ranged from 0.008-4 mcg/mL in clinical studies (n=35) and \le 0.004 - &gt; 4 mcg/mL in surveillance studies (n=1031).*
L695: *This Reviewer agrees with the Applicant proposed MIC BPs of \le 0.25(S)/0.5(I) \ge 1.0(R) mcg/mL for E. coli. Data from surveillance studies, clinical studies, and PK/PD analysis together support this BPs.*
L696: *In a follow up communication received from the Applicant on 6/7/2017, the Agency accepts the Applicant-provided recalculation of disk diffusion BPs of S/I/R as \ge \frac{(b)}{(a)}/19-\frac{(b)}{(a)} \le 18 mm for*
L697: Name of the Product: Baxdela

L701: ## ***Klebsiella pneumoniae***
L702: The Applicant proposed the following breakpoints for *K. pneumoniae*:
L703: Organism	MIC (\mug/mL)	Zone Diametera (mm)
K. pneumoniae	\le 0.25 Susceptible	\ge 19 Susceptible
0.5 Intermediate	19-18 Intermediate
\ge 1 Resistant	\le 18 Resistant
L704: MIC = minimum inhibitory concentration
L705: a Delafoxacin 5-\mug disk
L706: The following rationale was provided by the Applicant:
L707: - A susceptible breakpoint of \le 0.25 mcg/mL is predicted to cover 66.5% of contemporary *K. pneumoniae* isolates and 64.5% of contemporary *K. pneumoniae* isolates from skin and soft-tissue infections.
- Delafoxacin MIC distributions are bimodal for *K. pneumoniae* isolates. The proposed delafloxacin breakpoint of 0.25 mcg/mL would cover *K. pneumoniae* isolates in the first mode but would be resistant to isolates in the second mode as delafloxacin-resistant, where QRDR mutations and ESBLs would more likely occur.
- When microbiological outcomes were tabulated by MIC for clinical study isolates (MEFUI population Pool 1), 100% efficacy (17/17) of delafloxacin was observed for isolates up to a delafloxacin MIC of 4 mcg/mL.
- For *K. pneumoniae* clinical isolates, the isolates were distributed between 0.03 to 4 mcg/mL and had 100% microbiological response rates (MEFUI population, Pool 1).
- Efficacy of delafloxacin was demonstrated in a neutropenic mouse thigh model of infection with *K. pneumoniae* isolates with delafloxacin MIC values of 0.06 to mcg/mL.
- Based upon PK/PD target attainment for *E. coli*, both the IV (300 mg Q12h) and oral (450 mg Q12h) formulations, should provide sufficient exposure to cover *K. pneumoniae* isolates with MIC values of \le 0.25 mcg/mL.
- When microbiological outcomes were tabulated by MIC for *K. pneumoniae* clinical isolates, 100% eradication (17/17) of delafloxacin was observed for isolates up to a delafloxacin MIC of 4 \mug/mL.
L708: Reviewer's comment: As mentioned earlier, the clinical experience with *K. pneumoniae* and other indicated gram-negative pathogens was very limited in clinical studies. While *K. pneumoniae* was recovered as baseline from 22 subjects in delafloxacin arm in clinical studies, they were mostly associated with polymicrobial infections. All of them were eradicated at the end of treatment. The MIC values for these clinical isolates were as follows: 4 at 0.03 mcg/mL; 3 at 0.06 mcg/mL; 11 at 0.12 mcg/mL; 3 at 0.25 mcg/mL; and 1 at 4 mcg/mL. The MIC90 of delafloxacin against *K. pneumoniae* in clinical and surveillance studies were 0.25 and &gt;4
L709: Name of the Product: Baxdela
L710: (Delafoxacin 450 mg Tablets and IV 300 mg/vial)
L711: Date Review Completed: 06/15/2017
L712: *mcg/mL respectively. The MIC ranged from 0.03-4 mcg/mL in clinical studies (n=45) and \le 0.004 - &gt; 4 mcg/mL in surveillance studies (n=800).*
L713: *This Reviewer agrees with the Applicant proposed MIC BPs of \le 0.25(S)/0.5(I)/\ge 1.0(R) mcg/mL for E. coli. Data from surveillance studies, clinical studies, and PK/PD analysis together support this BPs.*

L744: ## APPLICANT'S PROPOSED LABELING
L745: Sections 1 and 12 of the package insert are shown below.
L746: ## 1 INDICATION AND USAGE
L747: ## 1.1 Acute Bacterial Skin and Skin Structure Infections
L748: BAXDELA is indicated for the treatment of acute bacterial skin and skin structure infections (ABSSSI) caused by susceptible isolates of the following Gram-positive organisms:
L749: *Staphylococcus aureus* (including methicillin-resistant [MRSA] and methicillin-susceptible [MSSA] isolates), *Staphylococcus haemolyticus*, *Staphylococcus lugdunensis*, *Streptococcus agalactiae*, *Streptococcus anginosus* Group (including *Streptococcus anginosus*, *Streptococcus intermedius*, and *Streptococcus constellatus*), *Streptococcus pyogenes*, and *Enterococcus faecalis*, and by the following Gram-negative organisms:
L750: *Escherichia coli*, *Enterobacter cloacae*, *Klebsiella pneumoniae*, and *Pseudomonas aeruginosa*.
L751: ## 12 CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY
L752: ## 12.1 Mechanism of Action
L753: BAXDELA is an antibacterial [see Clinical Pharmacology (12.4)].
L754: ## 12.4 Microbiology
L755: ## Mechanism of Action
L756: belongs to the fluoroquinolone class of antibacterial drugs and is anionic in nature. The antibacterial activity of is due to the inhibition of bacterial topoisomerase IV and DNA gyrase (topoisomerase II), enzymes required for bacterial DNA replication, transcription, repair, and recombination. exhibits concentration-dependent bactericidal activity against Gram-positive and Gram-negative bacteria in vitro

(Delafoxacin 450 mg Tablets and IV 300 mg/vial)

L760: (b) (4)
L761: ## **Resistance**
L762: Resistance to fluoroquinolones, including [REDACTED] (b) (4), can occur due to mutations in defined regions of the target bacterial enzymes topoisomerase IV and DNA gyrase referred to as Quinolone-Resistance Determining Regions (QRDRs), or through altered efflux.
L763: Fluoroquinolones, including [REDACTED] (b) (4), have a different chemical structure and mechanism of action relative to other classes of antibacterial compounds (e.g. aminoglycosides, macrolides, \beta-lactams, glycopeptides, tetracyclines, and oxazolidinones) (b) (4)
L764: (b) (4)
L765: In vitro resistance to delafloxacin develops (b) (4) by multiple step mutations. [REDACTED] (b) (4) delafloxacin-resistant mutants were selected in vitro at (b) (4) (10^{-9}) frequencies.
L766: Although cross-resistance between [REDACTED] (b) (4) and other fluoroquinolone-class antibacterial agents has been observed, some isolates resistant to other fluoroquinolone-class antibacterial agents may be susceptible to BAXDELA.
L767: ## **Interaction with Other Antimicrobial Compounds**
L768: In vitro drug combination studies with [REDACTED] (b) (4) and aztreonam, ceftazidime, colistin, daptomycin, linezolid, meropenem, tigecycline, trimethoprim/sulfamethoxazole, and vancomycin demonstrated neither synergy nor antagonism.

L808: S = susceptible; I = intermediate; R = resistant
L809: (b) (4)
L810: A report of “Susceptible” indicates that the antimicrobial drug is likely to inhibit growth of the pathogen if the antimicrobial drug reaches the concentration usually achievable at the site of infection. A report of “Intermediate” indicates that the result should be considered equivocal, and if the microorganism is not fully susceptible to alternative drugs, the test should be repeated. This category implies possible clinical (b) (4) in body sites where the drug is physiologically concentrated. This category also provides a buffer zone that prevents small uncontrolled technical factors from causing major discrepancies in interpretation. A report of “Resistant” indicates that the antimicrobial drug is not likely to inhibit growth of the pathogen if the antimicrobial drug reaches the concentrations usually achievable at the infection site; other therapy should be selected.

L1355: ## **Application Type: PDUFA V Application**
L1356: **INDICATIONS:** Delafloxacin is indicated for the treatment of acute bacterial skin and skin structure infections (ABSSSI) caused by susceptible isolates of the following Gram-positive organisms: *Staphylococcus aureus* (including methicillin-resistant [MRSA] and methicillin-susceptible [MSSA] isolates), *Staphylococcus haemolyticus*, *Staphylococcus lugdunensis*, *Streptococcus agalactiae*, *Streptococcus anginosus* Group, *Streptococcus pyogenes*, and *Enterococcus faecalis*, and by the following Gram-negative organisms: *Escherichia coli*, *Enterobacter cloacae*, *Klebsiella pneumoniae*, and *Pseudomonas aeruginosa*.
L1357: **Any Additional Clinical Microbiology Comments:** There are no additional comments.

L1368: Figure 1 displays four graphs showing the Log % of control/cell density versus Time (min) for de novo macromolecular synthesis in E. coli 25922, comparing Delafloxacin (Dela) and comparators (Cipro, CAM, RIF).
DNA synthesis: Delafloxacin (2xMIC Dela) and Ciprofloxacin (2xMIC Cipro) show significant inhibition, reducing cell density to approximately 10% of control by 50 minutes. Delafloxacin (2xMIC Dela) shows slightly greater inhibition than Ciprofloxacin (2xMIC Cipro).
Protein synthesis: Delafloxacin (2xMIC Dela) and Ciprofloxacin (2xMIC Cipro) show minimal inhibition, maintaining cell density near 100%. Chloramphenicol (2xMIC CAM) shows moderate inhibition, reducing cell density to approximately 50% of control by 50 minutes.
RNA Synthesis: Delafloxacin (2xMIC Dela) and Rifampin (5xMIC RIF) show significant inhibition. Rifampin (5xMIC RIF) shows greater inhibition, reducing cell density to approximately 10% of control by 50 minutes. Delafloxacin (2xMIC Dela) reduces cell density to approximately 50% of control by 50 minutes.
Cell-wall Synthesis: Delafloxacin (2xMIC Dela) shows significant inhibition, reducing cell density to approximately 50% of control by 50 minutes. Chloramphenicol (2xMIC CAM) shows minimal inhibition, maintaining cell density near 100%.
L1369: Figure 2 displays two bar charts showing the Delafloxacin MIC Distribution Against S. aureus from 2014-2015 Surveillance.
A: region (Left Chart): Shows the percentage of isolates (Y-axis, 0 to 100) versus MIC (\mug/mL) (X-axis, logarithmic scale: 0.004, 0.008, 0.015, 0.03, 0.06, 0.12, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16). Data is categorized by region: US (N=2103, black bars) and Europe (N=503, white bars). The US isolates show a high percentage (approx. 55%) at the lowest MIC (0.004 mcg/mL). The percentage drops sharply for higher MICs, with minimal isolates above 0.015 mcg/mL.
B: phenotype (Right Chart): Shows the percentage of isolates (Y-axis, 0 to 100) versus MIC (\mug/mL) (X-axis, logarithmic scale: 0.004, 0.008, 0.015, 0.03, 0.06, 0.12, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16). Data is categorized by phenotype: MSSA (N=1548, black bars) and MRSA (N=1058, white bars). MSSA isolates show a high percentage (approx. 75%) at the lowest MIC (0.004 mcg/mL). The percentage drops sharply for higher MICs, with minimal isolates above 0.015 mcg/mL. MRSA isolates show a much lower percentage at the lowest MIC (approx. 5%) and a distribution across higher MICs.
L1370: Figure 3 displays two bar charts showing the distribution of Delafoxacin Minimum Inhibitory Concentration (MIC) against Coagulase-negative Staphylococci (CoNS) from 2014-2015 surveillance studies.
A: region (Left Chart): Shows the percentage of isolates (Y-axis, 0 to 100%) versus MIC (\mug/mL) (X-axis, logarithmic scale: 0.004, 0.008, 0.015, 0.03, 0.06, 0.12, 0.25, 0.5, 1, >1). Data is stratified by region: US (N=438, black bars) and Europe (N=306, white bars). The US shows a higher percentage of isolates at lower MICs (0.004 and 0.008) compared to Europe.
B: phenotype (Right Chart): Shows the percentage of isolates (Y-axis, 0 to 100%) versus MIC (\mug/mL) (X-axis, logarithmic scale: 0.004, 0.008, 0.015, 0.03, 0.06, 0.12, 0.25, 0.5, 1, >1). Data is stratified by phenotype: MScCoNS (methicillin-susceptible coagulase-negative staphylococci, N=345, black bars) and MRCoNS (methicillin-resistant coagulase-negative staphylococci, N=399, white bars). MScCoNS shows a higher percentage of isolates at lower MICs (0.004 and 0.008) compared to MRCoNS.

L1372: Figure 5 displays two bar charts showing the distribution of Delafloxacin Minimum Inhibitory Concentration (MIC) against K. pneumoniae isolates from 2014-2015 surveillance.

Data is stratified by geographic region: US (N=458, black bars) and Europe (N=342, white bars). The distribution shows a peak around 0.06 \mu\text{g/mL} for both regions, with US isolates generally having slightly higher percentages in the lower MIC ranges.
B: phenotype (Right Chart): Shows the percentage of isolates (Y-axis, 0 to 100%) versus MIC values (\mu\text{g/mL}, X-axis, logarithmic scale: 0.004, 0.008, 0.015, 0.03, 0.06, 0.12, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16). Data is stratified by phenotype: non-ESBL (N=564, black bars) and ESBL (N=236, white bars). The distribution shows a peak around 0.06 \mu\text{g/mL} for non-ESBL isolates, and a peak around 0.12 \mu\text{g/mL} for ESBL isolates.

L1375: Figure 8 displays two bar charts showing the distribution of Minimum Inhibitory Concentration (MIC) values for delafloxacin against Enterobacter spp. isolates from 2014-2015 surveillance.
A: region (Left Chart): Shows MIC distribution by geographic region. The Y-axis represents the percentage of isolates (0 to 100%). The X-axis represents MIC values (\mug/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.004, 0.008, 0.015, 0.03, 0.12, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16). Data is shown for US (N=622, black bars) and Europe (N=241, white bars). The highest percentage of isolates falls in the 0.06 mcg/mL MIC range (US ~40%, Europe ~10%).
B: species (Right Chart): Shows MIC distribution by species. The Y-axis represents the percentage of isolates (0 to 100%). The X-axis represents MIC values (\mug/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.004, 0.008, 0.015, 0.03, 0.12, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16). Data is shown for E. cloacae (N=439, black bars) and E. aerogenes (N=223, white bars). The highest percentage of isolates falls in the 0.06 mcg/mL MIC range (E. cloacae ~45%, E. aerogenes ~35%).
L1376: Figure 9 displays two bar charts showing the Delafoxacin Minimum Inhibitory Concentration (MIC) distribution against P. aeruginosa isolates from 2014-2015 surveillance.
A: region (Left Chart): Shows the percentage of isolates (% of isolates) versus MIC (\mug/mL) for isolates from the US (N=147, black bars) and Europe (N=155, white bars). The MIC values range from <0.004 to >8. The US isolates show a higher percentage of isolates at higher MIC values (e.g., 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8) compared to Europe isolates, indicating higher resistance prevalence in the US.
B: phenotype (Right Chart): Shows the percentage of isolates (% of isolates) versus MIC (\mug/mL) for isolates categorized by phenotype: CAZ-S (ceftazidime-susceptible, N=245, black bars) and CAZ-R (ceftazidime-resistant, N=42, white bars). The CAZ-S isolates show a higher percentage of isolates at higher MIC values (e.g., 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8) compared to CAZ-R isolates, indicating higher resistance prevalence in CAZ-S isolates.
L1377: Figure 13: A graph showing the Probability of PK/PD Target Attainment (%) versus MIC (mcg/mL) for S. aureus isolates on Day 1 and Day 4. The graph includes a bar chart showing the Relative Frequency of the MIC Distribution (%). The legend indicates: Day 1 (solid line), Day 4 (dashed line), Net bacterial stasis (solid circles), 1 log10 CFU reduction (solid triangles), MRSA (n=563, dark bars), and MSSA (n=748, light bars). The MIC values range from <= 0.004 to 4 mcg/mL. The probability of target attainment is high (>95%) for MIC values up to 0.5 mcg/mL, dropping sharply thereafter.
L1378: Figure 14: A graph showing the Probability of Target Attainment (%), MIC Frequency (n), and MIC (ug/mL) for E. coli isolates. The X-axis is MIC (ug/mL) ranging from 0.008 to 8. The left Y-axis is Probability of fAUC24/MIC Target Attainment (%), ranging from 0 to 100. The right Y-axis is MIC Frequency (n), ranging from 0 to 50. Grey bars represent MIC frequencies. A solid line represents the probability for stasis (14.5), and a dashed line represents the probability for 1-Log10 Reduction (26.2).
L1379: Figure 15: A graph showing the Probability of Target Attainment (%), MIC Frequency (n), and MIC (ug/mL) for P. aeruginosa isolates. The X-axis is MIC (ug/mL) ranging from 0.015 to 8. The left Y-axis is Probability of fAUC24/MIC Target Attainment (%), ranging from 0 to 100. The right Y-axis is MIC Frequency (n), ranging from 0 to 16. Grey bars represent MIC frequencies. A solid line represents the probability for stasis (3.81), and a dashed line represents the probability for 1-Log10 Reduction (5.02).
L1380: Figure 16 is a scatter plot showing the correlation between Broth Minimum Inhibitory Concentration (MIC) values (Y-axis, logarithmic scale) and Disk Zone Diameter (X-axis, linear scale) for Delafoxacin (5 mcg) against evaluated target pathogens. The plot is labeled "Overall (n=918)a".
The regression equation is: \log_{10} y = -0.3252x + 4.8904
Statistical parameters are: R^2 = 0.8194 and R = -0.9052.
The Y-axis (MIC in \mu\text{g/mL}) ranges from <0.002 to >1. The X-axis (Zone Diameter in mm) ranges from 6 to 53.
Data points are clustered along the regression line, showing a strong negative correlation.

L1388: Figure G8 consists of two line graphs showing the Log10 cfu/mL of pouch exudate over time (hours post-infection) for MRSA 11540-infected Rat Granuloma Pouches treated with Delafoxacin (top) and Vancomycin (bottom).
The top graph (Delafoxacin treatment) shows three lines: Delafoxacin - pouch (green dashed line), Delafoxacin - CFU (blue solid line), and Untreated controls (red solid line). The Untreated controls show the highest bacterial load, peaking around 7.5 Log10 cfu/mL at 30 hours. The Delafoxacin - CFU line shows a significant reduction in bacterial load, dropping from approximately 5.0 Log10 cfu/mL at 30 hours to around 3.5 Log10 cfu/mL at 90 hours. The Delafoxacin - pouch line shows a slight increase in bacterial load over time, starting around 6.0 Log10 cfu/mL and reaching around 6.5 Log10 cfu/mL at 90 hours. A vertical line indicates the MIC (Minimum Inhibitory Concentration) of Delafoxacin, which is approximately 1000 ng/mL pouch exudate.
The bottom graph (Vancomycin treatment) shows two lines: Vancomycin - CFU (purple solid line) and Untreated controls (blue solid line). The Untreated controls show a high bacterial load, peaking around 7.5 Log10 cfu/mL at 30 hours. The Vancomycin - CFU line shows a significant reduction in bacterial load, dropping from approximately 6.0 Log10 cfu/mL at 30 hours to around 3.5 Log10 cfu/mL at 90 hours.
L1389: Figure G9 displays two graphs showing the effect of Delafoxacin (50 mg/kg SC, top) and Ciprofloxacin (50 mg/kg SC, bottom) on A. baumannii 1705943-infected Rat Granuloma Pouches over 100 hours. The Y-axis represents Log10 cfu/mL of pouch exudate (left scale, 0 to 9) and the concentration of the drug in ng/mL pouch exudate (right scale, logarithmic, 1 to 100,000). The MIC (Minimum Inhibitory Concentration) is indicated by a horizontal line at approximately 3.5 Log10 cfu/mL.
Top Graph (Delafoxacin): Untreated controls show high bacterial growth (around 7.5 Log10 cfu/mL). Delafoxacin treatment (blue line, CFU) shows a significant reduction in bacterial load, dropping below the MIC line by 50 hours and remaining low (around 2.5 Log10 cfu/mL) until 100 hours. The concentration of Delafoxacin in pouch exudate (purple line, pouch exudate) peaks around 20 hours (approx. 10,000 ng/mL) and then declines.
Bottom Graph (Ciprofloxacin): Untreated controls show high bacterial growth (around 7.5 Log10 cfu/mL). Ciprofloxacin treatment (blue line, CFU) shows a significant reduction in bacterial load, dropping below the MIC line by 50 hours and remaining low (around 2.5 Log10 cfu/mL) until 100 hours. The concentration of Ciprofloxacin in pouch exudate (green line, pouch exudate) peaks around 20 hours (approx. 5,000 ng/mL) and then declines.

L1391: Figure G11 displays two graphs showing the effect of Delafoxacin (15 mg/kg SC, top) and Ciprofloxacin (15 mg/kg SC, bottom) on P. aeruginosa PA01-infected Rat Granuloma Pouches over 110 hours.
The Y-axis represents Log10 CFU/mL pouch exudate (left scale, 0 to 9) and [Antibiotic] ng/mL pouch exudate (right scale, 1 to 100000, logarithmic scale). The X-axis represents Time (hours).
The MIC (Minimum Inhibitory Concentration) is indicated by a horizontal line on the right Y-axis.
Top Graph (Delafoxacin):
Delafoxacin - pouch (dashed purple line): Shows a transient increase in CFU around 30 hours, reaching approximately 6.5 Log10 CFU/mL, before declining.
Delafoxacin - CFU (solid blue line): Shows a significant decrease in CFU, dropping from approximately 5.5 Log10 CFU/mL at 10 hours to around 2.5 Log10 CFU/mL by 100 hours.
Untreated controls (solid red line): Maintain high CFU levels, around 7.5 Log10 CFU/mL.
Bottom Graph (Ciprofloxacin):
Ciprofloxacin - pouch (dashed green line): Shows a transient increase in CFU around 30 hours, reaching approximately 6.5 Log10 CFU/mL, before declining.
Ciprofloxacin - CFU (solid blue line): Shows a significant decrease in CFU, dropping from approximately 5.5 Log10 CFU/mL at 10 hours to around 2.5 Log10 CFU/mL by 100 hours.
Untreated controls (solid red line): Maintain high CFU levels, around 7.5 Log10 CFU/mL.

L1401: Figure A displays the Broth Versus Disk Correlation and Error-rate bounding Analysis for Delafoxacin against \beta-hemolytic Streptococci (n=188). The graph plots MIC (\mug/mL) on the Y-axis (log scale, 0.002 to >32) against Zone Diameter (mm) on the X-axis (6 to 53). The proposed interpretive criteria are shown as dashed lines: R (Resistant) MIC \ge 8 \mug/mL, S (Susceptible) MIC \le 0.25 \mug/mL, and I (Intermediate) MIC 0.5 to 7.9 \mug/mL. The regression equation is \log_2 Y = -0.1166X - 2.3743, with R^2 = 0.1842 and R = -0.4292. The data points are distributed across the R, S, and I categories.
L1402: Figure B displays the Broth Versus Disk Correlation and Error-rate bounding Analysis for Delafoxacin against \beta-hemolytic Streptococci (n=96). The graph plots MIC (\mug/mL) on the Y-axis (log scale, 0.002 to >32) against Zone Diameter (mm) on the X-axis (6 to 53). The proposed interpretive criteria are shown as dashed lines: R (Resistant) MIC \ge 8 \mug/mL, S (Susceptible) MIC \le 0.25 \mug/mL, and I (Intermediate) MIC 0.5 to 7.9 \mug/mL. The regression equation is \log_2 Y = -0.1103X - 2.3909, with R^2 = 0.2149 and R = -0.4636. The data points are distributed across the R, S, and I categories.
L1403: Figure A is a scatter plot showing the correlation between MIC (µg/mL) on the Y-axis and Zone Diameter (mm) on the X-axis for the S. anginosus Group (n=134). The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from <0.004 to >32. The X-axis ranges from 6 to 53 mm. The plot displays the R and S breakpoints. The regression equation is \log y = -0.1113x + 2.5441, with R^2 = 0.2452 and R = -0.4952.

L1408: P. aeruginosa (n=24)
\log_2 y = -0.2566x + 5.0864
R^2 = 0.9093
R = -0.9536
Graph showing MIC (µg/mL) on the Y-axis (log scale) versus Zone Diameter (mm) on the X-axis. The graph displays the relationship between MIC and Zone Diameter for P. aeruginosa (n=24). The Y-axis is labeled MIC (µg/mL) with values ranging from \le 0.004 to 4. The X-axis is labeled Zone Diameter (mm) with values ranging from 6 to 53. The graph shows a linear regression line and data points clustered around the line. The zones of resistance (R), intermediate (I), and susceptible (S) are indicated by dashed lines.
